In 1950, the Maracanã Stadium hosted 8 matches, including all but one of the host team's matches. The Maracanã was also the location of the tournament-deciding match between [Uruguay](679782.md) and [Brazil](149286.md), where Brazil only needed a draw to win the final group stage and the whole tournament. Brazil ended up losing 2–1 in front of a home crowd of more than 199,000. In 2014, the Maracanã hosted seven matches, including [the final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_Final), where [Germany](250204.md) beat [Argentina](454699.md) 1–0.[^cite_note-443]



[Barra Olympic Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barra_Olympic_Park), built for [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Summer_Olympics)

On 2 October 2009, the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md) selected Rio de Janeiro to host the [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-444] Rio made their first bid for the [1936 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936_Summer_Olympics), but lost to Berlin. They later made bids for the [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bids_for_the_2004_Summer_Olympics) and [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bids_for_the_2012_Summer_Olympics) Games, but failed to become a candidate city both times. Those games were awarded to [Athens](1216.md) and [London](17867.md) respectively.[^cite_note-GB_2-445] Rio is the first Brazilian and South American city to host the Summer Olympics. Rio de Janeiro also became the first city in the [southern hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere) outside of Australia to host the games – [Melbourne](17306237.md) in [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1956_Summer_Olympics) and [Sydney](27862.md) in [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympics). In July 2007, Rio successfully organized and hosted the [XV Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Pan_American_Games). Rio de Janeiro also hosted the [2011 Military World Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Military_World_Games) from 15 to 24 July 2011. The 2011 [Military World Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_World_Games) were the largest military sports event ever held in Brazil, with approximately 4,900 athletes from 108 countries competing in 20 sports.[^cite_note-446] Rio de Janeiro hosted the 2016 Olympics and Paralympics. The Olympic Games were held from 5 to 21 August 2016. The Paralympics were held from 7 to 18 September 2016.

The city has a history as host of major international sports events. The [Ginásio do Maracanãzinho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gin%C3%A1sio_do_Maracan%C3%A3zinho) was the host arena for the official [FIBA Basketball World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Basketball_World_Championship) for its [1954](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_FIBA_World_Championship) and [1963](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_FIBA_World_Championship) editions. Later, the [Jacarepaguá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aut%C3%B3dromo_Internacional_Nelson_Piquet) circuit in Rio de Janeiro was the site for the [Formula One](10854.md) [Brazilian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Grand_Prix) from 1978 to 1989. Rio de Janeiro also hosted the [MotoGP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prix_motorcycle_racing) Brazilian Grand Prix from 1995 to 2004 and the [Champ Car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Championship_Auto_Racing_Teams) event from 1996 to 1999. [WCT/WQS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Surfing_Professionals) [surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surfing) championships were contested on the beaches from 1985 to 2001. The [Rio Champions Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Champions_Cup) Tennis tournament is held in the spring. As part of its preparations to host the [2007 Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Pan_American_Games), Rio built a new stadium, [Estádio Olímpico João Havelange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A1dio_Ol%C3%ADmpico_Jo%C3%A3o_Havelange), to hold 45,000 people. It was named after Brazilian ex-[FIFA](11049.md) president [João Havelange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Havelange). The city of Rio de Janeiro owns the stadium, but it was rented to [Botafogo de Futebol e Regatas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botafogo_de_Futebol_e_Regatas) for 20 years.[^cite_note-rent-447] Rio de Janeiro has also a multi-purpose arena, the [HSBC Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC_Arena_\(Rio_de_Janeiro\)).

The Brazilian [martial art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_art) [capoeira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capoeira) is very popular. Other popular sports are [basketball](3921.md), [beach football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beach_soccer), [beach volleyball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beach_volleyball), Beach American Football, [footvolley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Footvolley), [surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surfing), [kite surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kite_surfing), [hang gliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hang_gliding), [motor racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_racing), [Brazilian Jiu-Jitsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Jiu-Jitsu), [Luta Livre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luta_Livre), [sailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sailing_\(sport\)), and competitive [rowing](25736.md). Another sport that is highly popular on the beaches of Rio is called *[frescobol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frescobol)* (pronounced [\[fɾeʃkoˈbɔw\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Portuguese)), a type of beach tennis. Rio de Janeiro is also a popular location for [Rock climbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_climbing), with hundreds of routes all over the city, ranging from easy boulders to technical climbs. [Sugarloaf Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarloaf_Mountain_\(Brazil\)) is an example, with routes from the easy third grade (American 5.4, French 3) to the extremely difficult ninth grade (5.13/8b), up to 280 m (919 ft).
